## 1. The owner's question, answered from the code and from live data

**Question (2026-09-06):** *"sessions across questions … if AI initially returns huge detailed in second and third turn how it will get handled … is this correct and accurate, or do we need fixes for the AI prompt and tool search as well?"*

**Answer: the description was accurate as CODE behaviour; two of the three defects have not yet fired on live data because every live answer so far is short; the third (stale citation numbers) is a hazard, not a measured defect. The prompt lane and the tool-search lane each have their own defects and need work too — §3 and §4.**

### 1.1 What happens today, turn by turn (verified, file:line)

### 1.2 The numbers that matter for "a huge first answer"

| Quantity | Value | Consequence |
|---|---|---|
| `MaxCompletionTokens` | 1,500 tokens per model turn (`BusinessSearchSettings.cs:75`, appsettings) | an English prose/table answer of 1,500 tokens is ≈ 4,500–7,000 characters; Gujarati/Hindi ≈ 1,500–3,000 characters (more tokens per character) |
| `SessionTurnMaxChars` | 4,000 (class default = appsettings) | a COMPLETE English answer above 4,000 chars is stored cut, mid-sentence, with no mark; the screen replay (M4 §08a) then shows a truncated answer as if finished; the model is replayed a truncated answer as if finished |
| `FollowUpTurns` | 6 → 12 turns replayed | worst case 12 × 4,000 = 48,000 chars ≈ 12,000 tokens of history on EVERY iteration of EVERY follow-up |
| iterations × history | up to 4 model turns per answer | a deep follow-up records ≈ 40–80k tokens into the 100k/min BUSINESS window (AG13-05 arithmetic; AG1-03 reproduction) → the 2nd–3rd colleague in the same minute gets "asking too quickly" for their FIRST question, after their daily unit was spent |
| Live data (2026-09-06, both regions) | CA: 21 sessions / 25 assistant turns, **max assistant turn 442 chars**, p50 143, p90 411; 21 of 25 assistant turns carry `[n]` markers; 4 sessions have two exchanges; largest session document 1,160 bytes. IN: 2 sessions, max 147 chars, no follow-ups | **the 4,000-char cut has never fired on live data** (answers are short so far); stale-number replay is REACHABLE (markers are in 84% of stored answers) but no follow-up has yet re-cited one; the session store is tiny today, so the fix in §2 costs nothing measurable at today's sizes |

**So, is the sentence in my update correct?** Point by point: "every stored turn is cut to 4,000 characters" — TRUE (code), not yet exercised live. "the last 6 exchanges are replayed" — TRUE. "tool results and sources are never replayed; each follow-up re-searches" — TRUE. "a long but complete first answer is stored cut with no marker, so the replay on screen shows a truncated answer" — TRUE in code, unfired live. "a long partial answer loses its partial marker" — TRUE in code (AG7-D1), unfired live. "the previous answer's citation numbers are replayed although the numbers restart at 1" — TRUE that they are replayed; whether the model then cites a stale number is **NOT MEASURED** (§2.3 A3 says how to measure it before shipping the strip).

**Do the prompt and tool search also need work?** Yes. Tool search (§3): the catalogue tool asks for the phone's 5-row page and cuts silently; a lost catalogue leg is reported as a complete search; a one-person business is offered the team tool; ten Group B tools emit citations for rows the payload cap removed; money reaches the model as bare numbers; an unnamed member is named by e-mail; a failed roster read reads as "not on the team"; the knowledge fan-out is cut by the phone's 4-second dial. Prompt (§4): the answer language is the UI locale, not the question's language (a promise in PLAN §18.7 and decision 14); the fixed refusal sentence carries a clause the approved sheet forbids; two model-facing instructions disagree about the catalogue query field; no rule for two colleagues with the same first name; the business token-window refusal uses the member's "too quickly" sentence. None of the measured wording needs to change (§4.3 lists what is measured and must stay).

## 1B. THE THREE FLOWS — LOCKED DESIGN (plain words; brainstormed with the owner 2026-09-06)

> ‼️ **This section wins.** Where §2.3, §3.3 or §4.4 propose something different (a new dial, an extra
> mechanism), THIS section is what P5B codes. The detailed sections stay for the evidence and the tests.
> Status: ‼️ CONFIRMED BY THE OWNER IN CHAT, 2026-09-06 (all three flows) — recorded as PLAN §15 decisions 26, 27, 28.

### Flow 1 — one conversation, many questions

**Today's problem in one line:** what is stored is cut to 4,000 letters with no mark, the "cut short" mark is lost on exactly the answers that need it, and old source numbers are replayed to the model.

**The locked rules (5):**

| # | Rule | What it replaces |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **What the member saw is what is stored.** The whole answer, never cut. The store adds the "cut short" mark itself when the answer ended early — including when an error hit after text had started. The model's own answer limit (1,500 tokens) is the only bound; no new dial. | the 4,000 cut at store time; the controller gluing the mark on; the proposed `SessionStoredTurnMaxChars` dial (dropped) |
| 2 | **What the model is reminded of is the last 6 exchanges**, each answer trimmed to 4,000 letters, old source numbers `[1] [2]` removed, the "cut short" mark kept. The two existing dials (`FollowUpTurns` 6, `SessionTurnMaxChars` 4,000) are the only cost levers; no new dial. Worst case ≈ 12k tokens of history per model call. | `SessionTurnMaxChars` meaning "stored"; the proposed `SessionReplayMaxChars` dial (dropped) |
| 3 | **Sources and tool results are never stored or replayed.** Every follow-up searches again and re-checks access. (unchanged — keep) | — |
| 4 | **A question the platform fails to answer costs nothing.** The business's busy-minute check runs BEFORE the daily units are charged and answers with its own sentence and a retry time; an answer that fails after the charge (search down, timed out) gives both units back. | the silent charge; the member-pace sentence for a business-wide limit |
| 5 | **Exchanges are stored in the order they were asked; a Recent row reopens, it never re-asks.** | the stop-then-ask inversion; the header-panel re-ask that charged twice (live) |

**Dropped as not worth the code:** carrying the session ETag from the first read (1 RU per ask — accepted residual).
**One small probe, not a study:** after rule 2 lands, 10 real follow-ups ("tell me more about the second one…") to confirm no stale number is cited. Recorded as numbers in AUDIT-P5B.

### Flow 1 in plain words (the owner's questions, 2026-09-06: "is this best practice, what does it cost, what exactly do we store and what do we send the AI again?")

**What is stored.** Only the words: each question and each answer, exactly as the member saw them. One record per conversation, per member, inside the business's own data, kept 7 days after the last question. Nothing else — no documents, no search results, no source cards. (Today: the same, but the answer is cut at 4,000 letters without saying so.)

**What is sent to the AI on a follow-up.** The AI remembers nothing between calls, so every call carries: (1) the standing instructions and the list of tools — the same every time, unavoidable; (2) the last 6 questions and answers of THIS conversation, each answer trimmed to 4,000 letters, old source numbers removed, the "cut short" mark kept; (3) the new question. NOT the documents or the earlier search results: the AI searches again for the new question and access is re-checked every time.

**Why store whole but send trimmed.** The member who reopens a conversation wants the whole answer. The AI only needs enough to follow the thread. Storing costs nothing; sending costs tokens on every call.

**Cost.** Storage: about 1 KB per conversation today, ~50 KB in the worst case — negligible. Tokens: a typical follow-up adds about 1–2k tokens per model call (live answers are 150–450 letters); the worst case is 12k per call, and a hard answer makes up to 4 calls. The two existing dials (6 exchanges, 4,000 letters) cap it — no new dial. The business allowance is 100,000 tokens a minute: dozens of ordinary questions, or two follow-ups on a very long conversation.

**Is it best practice?** Yes. This is the standard chat pattern: keep the full transcript for the person, replay a bounded recent window to the model, never carry retrieved content across turns so permissions apply fresh each time, and remove markers that mean nothing in the next turn. The alternatives — summarising old turns with an extra AI call, or storing the source cards with the answer — cost more (a call per turn; a schema change and a permission gap) for no gain at this size.

**Other implications.** A failed answer no longer costs the member a unit (one extra small write, only on failure). No schema change, no migration, no new setting. The phone assistant is untouched. Reopening a conversation shows the whole last answer instead of a silently cut one.

### Flow 2 — how a question reaches the business's data

**Today's problem in one line:** the catalogue answer is the phone's 5-row page cut silently, a lost half of the catalogue is hidden, a one-person business is offered a team tool, ten tools send cards the answer never cites, money and names reach the model raw.

**The locked rules (6):**

| # | Rule | What it replaces |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Every list answer says total, shown, the rows, and "this is a sample" when shown < total — through ONE helper used by all 17 tools.** Sources are minted only for the rows the model actually read. Catalogue page = 25 rows (the index already fetches 26); the phone keeps 5. | `Bound` + `CapPayload` (Group B) vs `SerializeCapped` (Group A) — two shapes; the 5-row cut |
| 2 | **Anything that could not be read says "could not be checked", never "nothing found".** A lost catalogue leg → partial note (both legs lost → unavailable); a failed team-list read → unavailable, never "no such colleague"; a category-name lookup failure never fails a good answer; a business with no profile yet → "not set up yet", not "try again later". | the merge that hid a lost leg; the roster failure read as "not on the team" |
| 3 | **Every amount is formatted for the reader before the model sees it** (symbol from the currency, grouping from the reader's language), the currency code beside it — one helper for all tools. | five tools sending `24999` + `CAD` |
| 4 | **A person is shown by name, never by e-mail; a role by its localized name.** One name rule shared by the roster, the team tool and the analytics redactor. | the e-mail fallback in the team tool; raw role keys |
| 5 | **A one-person business gets no team tool and no member parameter**, and is never told the team topic is "not set up". | the team tool offered to a solo owner |
| 6 | **The screen search has its own time budget** — one dial `BusinessSearch:Retrieval:TimeoutSeconds` (default 8, measured on the largest business first), separate from the phone's 4 s. A slow part gives a partial note, not a failure. | the dead per-leg dial; the phone's budget governing the screen |

**Cost, same batch (no design change):** one scan per catalogue miss and one group count per question; customers = count + newest 10, never the whole list; one picture minted once per answer.
**Owner rulings still needed (§15):** reviews follow the page rule (Q6), AE → AED (Q10), minimum-hours on the index (Q11, zero live cost today).

### Flow 3 — what the model is told

**Today's problem in one line:** the model answers in the app's language instead of the question's, repeats a refusal sentence the approved sheet forbids, and blames a member for a business-wide limit.

**The locked rules (4):**

| # | Rule | What it replaces |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **The answer is in the language the question was asked in.** Gujarati or Hindi script decides; a Latin-script question follows the app language. The cards, statuses and money grouping stay in the app language. ‼️ The "Answer in …" line moves to the USER turn (beside the alphabet block) so the cached beginning of the prompt never changes with the question (§1C K-3). | "Answer in {UI language}" in the system message |
| 2 | **The two fixed sentences the model repeats are plain copy, ×5 languages:** no-access = "You don't have access to that." (the "ask the business owner" clause goes); busy business = its own sentence with a retry time. | the clause M5 forbids; the member-pace sentence |
| 3 | **Two colleagues with the same first name → the model asks which one** (one added line in the PEOPLE rule). | nothing |
| 4 | **Measured wording is not edited.** The one contradiction (alphabet block says "translate the question", catalogue rule says "send only the words") is settled by ONE 24-question probe on a two-alphabet business, and only re-worded if the probe fails. | — |

**Prompt cost:** everything is re-sent on every model call (up to 4 per answer); with Flow 1 rule 2 the history part is bounded at 12k tokens; the business window stays 100,000 tokens/minute and the class default is corrected to match.

### What P5B codes from this section — the checklist
Flow 1: rules 1, 2, 4, 5 (rule 3 unchanged) + the 10-follow-up probe. Flow 2: rules 1–6 + the three cost items + the three rulings. Flow 3: rules 1–3 + the probe in rule 4. Everything else in §5–§7 follows in the §16 order.


## 1C. ‼️ PROMPT CACHING — EXTREMELY IMPORTANT, A MUST (owner, 2026-09-06: "cost and performance is key")

**How it works, simply.** Azure OpenAI caches automatically: when two calls begin with the identical text, the shared beginning is served from cache — faster and billed at a discount. Nothing is "sent" to use it; the identical beginning IS the key. The prompt has to be at least about 1,000 tokens long to qualify (ours is ~5k+ with the 17 tools, so it qualifies), and the cache lasts a few minutes between calls. The code already reads the cache count back (`prompt_tokens_details.cached_tokens`, `BusinessSearchModelClient.cs:180`) and logs it on every answer (`BusinessSearchAgent.cs:657` "prompt … (cached …)"), and the cost estimate already prices cached tokens lower.

**What the order of the prompt is today, and what that means for the cache:** instructions → tools → conversation history → new question. The cache serves everything up to the FIRST thing that differs from the previous call. So anything that changes near the beginning throws away the whole cache for that call.

**Is there a bug that stops it?** Not one that switches it off — three things make it MISS when it should hit:

| # | Cause of a miss | Where | Who is hit | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| K-1 | **The tool list changes with the language the question was written in.** The search tools carry one field per alphabet, and the ASKED script is always added and moved FIRST (`BusinessSearchScriptPlan.Build`: `wanted = [asked] + alphabets`, ordered asked-first). English question → `[queryInEnglish]`; the next question in Gujarati → `[queryInGujarati, queryInEnglish]`. The tools sit at the beginning of the prompt, so the whole cache misses. This is the P4 leftover recorded as PLAN decision 22 and MEASUREMENTS-P4 §"the move does not finish the job" — never fixed | `clinqetcore/Interfaces/BusinessSearch/BusinessSearchScriptPlan.cs:50-70`; `BusinessSearchQueryRenderings.BuildSchema` | every member who asks in Gujarati/Hindi, and every two-alphabet business on every language switch | **The tool list depends only on the BUSINESS, never on the question:** fields = the business's alphabets (∪ Latin), in one canonical order, never reordered. The asked script is told to the model on the USER turn (the alphabet block already is), and a question in a script the business does not index still searches that script through the existing fallback (`Parse` substitutes the question for the asked script — no field needed). The field DESCRIPTIONS stay byte-identical (measured wording untouched); only which fields exist and their order becomes stable. ‼️ Re-measure compliance exactly as decision 22 demands (25/25 gate) — the field set is model-facing |
| K-2 | **The PEOPLE list may come back in a different order** each time the 60-second roster cache refreshes: the members query has no ORDER BY (`MemberLifecycleService.ListMembersAsync` — `.Where(...)…ToListAsync`, no ordering found; SQL does not guarantee row order). A reordered list = a different prompt beginning | `BusinessSearchRosterService.TryListAsync`; `BusinessSearchAgent.AppendPeopleAsync:626-630` | every business with 2–60 members, every 60 s | Sort the roster deterministically in the roster service (by display name, then membership id) before it is cached; one `OrderBy`, one test |
| K-3 | **Flow 3 rule 1 must not put the answer language in the system message.** "Answer in Gujarati" / "Answer in English" changes per question for a bilingual member; in the system message it would break the cache exactly as the alphabet block did before P4 C1 | `BusinessSearchAgent.cs:479-481` | design correction to §1B Flow 3 rule 1 | The answer-language line goes on the USER turn beside the alphabet block, not in the system message |
| K-4 | **Nobody has measured the hit rate.** P4's probe was below the cache minimum and read `cached_tokens: 0` for both variants; no live number exists | `MEASUREMENTS-P4 §"What this run does NOT claim"` | — | P5B measures on dev with real prompts: 10 follow-ups within 2 minutes, log `cached_tokens / prompt_tokens` per call, before and after K-1/K-2/K-3. Target: calls 2–4 inside one answer ≥ 80 % cached; a follow-up ≥ 60 % cached. Numbers in AUDIT-P5B |

**What ALREADY helps the cache and must be kept:** the alphabet block on the user turn (P4 C1); history that only grows at the end; deterministic trimming and stripping of old turns (Flow 1 rule 2 — the same input always produces the same text); ACCESS / NOT SET UP / WORK RULES that depend on the role, not the question; tools listed in a fixed (DI) order; no date or time anywhere in the instructions (verified: none).

**What cannot be helped:** when the 6-exchange window slides (from the 7th exchange on) the oldest turn drops off and the beginning changes — one miss, then hits again. A cold start after a few idle minutes is a miss by nature.

**P5B does K-1, K-2, K-3 in Batch 1 (they are cheap) and K-4 as the batch's measurement.** Status: ‼️ CONFIRMED BY THE OWNER IN CHAT, 2026-09-06 ("this definitely needs the fix") — PLAN §15 decision 29. ‼️ P5B's AUDIT-P5B is NOT accepted without the K-4 numbers.

| ID | Sev | Defect | Evidence | Live? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A-1 | High | A complete answer longer than `SessionTurnMaxChars` (4,000) is stored cut with no mark; the on-screen replay and the model replay both present it as finished | `McpSessionService.cs:119-133` `Content = content.Length > maxChars ? content[..maxChars] : content`; nothing marks the cut | not yet (max live turn 442 chars) |
| A-2 | High | The partial marker is appended BEFORE the cut, so the one answer that most needs it (a long, cut-short one) loses it; the client's `stripPartialMarker` then finds nothing and the "cut short — ask it again" state (M4 §05b) never shows | `BusinessSearchController.cs:464` `answer + PartialAnswerMarker` → `McpSessionService.cs:128` cut; AG7-D1; the only pin uses a 54-char answer (`BusinessSearchControllerIntegrationTests.cs:878-905`) | not yet |
| A-3 | Medium (hazard) | Stale `[n]` markers from the previous answer are replayed to the model; the new answer's numbers restart at 1; a re-cited stale number resolves to the WRONG card or to a dead pill | `BusinessSearchAgent.cs:523-532`; registry `new` per answer :101; 21/25 live answers carry markers | reachable, not measured |
| A-4 | Medium | An Error frame written AFTER deltas (C6 unavailable, generic failure) carries no `Partial`, so half an answer is stored as complete and the client draws the full footer under a red box | `BusinessSearchAgent.cs:298-308, 344-361`; controller persists `partial:false` (:167-171, :202); AG13-13 | reachable |
| A-5 | Medium | A question the platform FAILS to answer (token window, C6, timeout, generic 500 inside the stream) has already spent the member's 60 and the business's 200; no decrement exists | AG1-03; `EnforceLimitsAsync` runs before `StreamAsync`; repository has Increment only | reachable |
| A-6 | Medium | The per-BUSINESS token window (100k/min) refuses with the per-MEMBER "asking a little too quickly" sentence, no Retry-After, and it can fire at iteration ≥2 after tools already ran | `BusinessSearchAgent.cs:174-181` uses `BusinessSearch_Error_TooFast`; AG13-05 | reachable; deep follow-ups make it likely |
| A-7 | Medium | Replay cost is unbounded by anything but 12 × 4,000 chars; every iteration re-sends it; two deep follow-ups by two colleagues can exhaust the business minute | arithmetic in §1.2; AG1-03/AG13-05 | reachable |
| A-8 | Low | Stop-then-ask (or ask #2 while #1 streams) can persist the cancelled exchange AFTER the newer one, inverting order in `history` — the replay shows the wrong "last" exchange and the model reads the turns out of order | `PersistTurnAsync` on `CancellationToken.None` (:465-471) after the abort, while ask #2 has already read the session (:133); AG7 §2 "Stop-then-ask", AG13-19 | reachable |
| A-9 | Low | Two live sessions hold the same question twice: a re-ask path charges a unit for what should be a reopen | header-panel Recent rows call `hand(question)` → `ask()` (`AskHeaderButton.jsx:211`, `BusinessSearchPage.jsx:166`); Try again re-submits the OLD `asked` (`BusinessSearchPage.jsx:622`, mobile `index.tsx:602`); AG8a-D2/D3; live sessions `97faa9c1…`, `83045d80…` | **live, proven** |
| A-10 | Low | Session is read twice per ask (controller point read, then the CAS loop re-reads); the first ETag is discarded | `BusinessSearchController.cs:133` + `McpSessionService.cs:62`; AG4-06 | live, 1 RU/ask |
| A-11 | Info | Mobile classifies a stall as `error_0` in analytics where web says `stalled`; ping every 15 s vs client stall 20 s leaves a 5 s margin; no per-tool timeout (a slow Cosmos work query runs to the 150 s answer clock) | AG13-14, AG13-21 | design notes |

### 2.2 What is already right and must be kept (verified)

Sessions are per MEMBER inside the business partition (`Metadata["membershipId"]` stamped on write, required on read — a colleague's id or a foreign business gets "new conversation", never a 403). Every write is ETag CAS, `Gone` is terminal, the loop is bounded (4, jittered). Only prose is stored — never tool payloads, never citations — and every turn re-authorizes from the token (roles can change between turns; a document restricted between turns vanishes from turn 2). `Trim` keeps the newest `FollowUpTurns×2` turns in pairs. TTL is the real Cosmos `ttl`, 7 days sliding from the last turn. The client-supplied id is validated to the minted shape before it becomes a document id. (`McpSessionService.cs`, `AiSessionRepository.cs`, `BusinessSearchController.cs:117,133-136`; pinned by `McpSessionServiceTests`, `AiSessionRepositoryTests`, `BusinessSearchControllerIntegrationTests` session cases.)

### 2.3 The fix design (proposed — the owner asked to see the solution before it is coded; items marked ⚑ are decisions listed in §15)

**A1+A2 — store whole, mark at the store, cap at replay.**
- `clinqetshared/DTOs/AI/AIAssistantDtos.cs` `ConversationTurn`: add `public bool Partial { get; set; }` (in-memory DTO; **not** a Cosmos field — `AiConversationTurn` on the entity is untouched, so no §0.7).
- `McpSessionService`: `PartialAnswerMarker` becomes `public const` here (the store owns the marker); `AppendTo` stores the content WHOLE up to a storage guard, then appends the marker when `turn.Partial` OR when the guard cut the text. Storage guard ⚑ **Option 1 (recommended):** a new dial `BusinessSearch:SessionStoredTurnMaxChars` (default 12000 — above any 1,500-token answer in any of the five languages; class default = appsettings; the mirror test `TheAppsettingsBlock_MirrorsTheClassDefaults` covers it automatically). **Option 2 (cheaper, weaker):** keep 4,000 as the storage cap but append the marker AFTER the cut and treat any cut as partial — the replay stays truncated but is at least labelled "cut short". Cost of Option 1: a session document grows to at most 12 × ~12.5 KB ≈ 150 KB (today's largest is 1.2 KB), and a Cosmos replace charges by size, so a long-running session's per-ask write could reach the low hundreds of RU; at today's sizes the difference is zero. The owner picks.
- `BusinessSearchController.PersistTurnAsync`: passes `Partial = partial`, stops concatenating the marker; keeps `internal const string PartialAnswerMarker = McpSessionService.PartialAnswerMarker;` so the existing integration test still compiles and keeps pinning the wire value shared with `askRules.js:67` / `askRules.ts` (`PARTIAL_MARKER`).
- `BusinessSearchAgent.BuildMessagesAsync`: replays each turn capped to `SessionTurnMaxChars` (4,000) — the existing dial keeps its name and value, only its meaning moves from "stored" to "replayed"; a stored turn that ends with the marker keeps the marker after the cap (re-appended), because the model is MEANT to read it ("the model reads it back on the next turn", controller comment :43-45).
- Tests (API unit, `McpSessionServiceTests` + `BusinessSearchAgentTests`; integration `BusinessSearchControllerIntegrationTests`): a 5,000-char complete answer is stored whole and replayed as 4,000 chars; a 5,000-char partial answer is stored with the marker at its END; sabotage = swap the two lines back (cut then mark) → the marker test goes red. Client: no change needed (`GetSession` still returns content + marker; both hooks already strip it).

**A3 — strip stale citation markers from replayed assistant turns.**
- In `BuildMessagesAsync`, for `role == assistant`, replace `\[(\d{1,2})\]` with nothing before replay (the `[[partial-answer]]` marker is left alone — different shape). The stored text is untouched, so the on-screen replay keeps its grey inert numbers (M9 #10).
- ⚑ **This changes what the model is shown, so measure it before it ships** (rule 5 in §0): the scripted model client is NOT enough; use the live `gpt-5.6-luna` deployment as P4.5/P4.7 did (`findings/MEASUREMENTS-P4.5-2026-09-04.md` describes the loop; credentials come from the API appsettings at runtime, never copied). Protocol: 25 two-turn conversations on SX3SG2 (CA) — turn 1 a catalogue or document question that yields ≥3 citations; turn 2 one of "tell me more about the second one", "and what about that one?", "which of those is cheapest?" — count answers that contain a `[n]` whose n is NOT among turn 2's own tool citations. Report before/after. If "before" is already 0/25, the strip is still correct (it removes tokens that can only mislead) but the owner may rule it unnecessary.

**A4 — an Error after deltas is partial.** `BusinessSearchAgent.cs` C6 branch (:298-308) and the generic branch (:344-361): set `Partial = wroteAnswer` on the Error frame; the controller already forwards `frame.Partial` (:167) so the stored turn gets the marker and the clients switch to the "cut short" footer they already have. Test: scripted model streams two deltas then a tool that throws → assert the Error frame's `partial == true` and the stored turn ends with the marker.

**A5 — give the units back when the platform fails.** `IBusinessSearchUsageCounter` gains `RefundBusinessAsync/RefundMemberAsync` (atomic `PatchOperation.Increment("/count", -1)`, no-op on 404, never below 0 — use a read-check or a conditional patch); the controller tracks `sawError && answer.Length == 0` over the frames and refunds both units it charged (only those with a limit > 0) after the stream ends normally. NOT refunded: client cancel (tokens were spent), a partial answer, or a refusal (nothing was charged). Integration test (real Cosmos emulator, §0.8 money/counter class): a scripted C6 failure leaves both counters at their pre-ask value; sabotage = remove the refund call → red.

**A6 — the business window gets its own sentence and a Retry-After.** New key ×5 `BusinessSearch_Error_BusinessTooBusy` ("Your business has asked a lot in the last minute. Please try again shortly." + es/fr/gu/hi); the agent's token-window Error frame carries `RetryAfterSeconds` from the limiter (`RateLimitResult.RetryAfter`) in a new frame field ⚑ (wire-only DTO field, not schema) that both clients already know how to render for the 429 path (`retryAfterSeconds` → amber box + "Try again in N min"); the limiter check moves BEFORE the daily spend (call `CheckTokenRateLimitAsync` in the controller before `EnforceLimitsAsync`, or refund via A5). Mobile/web: read `retryAfterSeconds` off the Error frame like the 429 body.

**A7 — bound the replay.** ⚑ New dial `BusinessSearch:SessionReplayMaxChars` (default 16000 ≈ 4k tokens) applied from the newest turn backwards on top of the per-turn cap; the window stays `FollowUpTurns×2` turns. This bounds the history part of every iteration's prompt; the owner may prefer to lower `FollowUpTurns` instead (cheaper, loses context depth). Either way the SKILL §8/§9 sentences change to state the real per-follow-up budget.

**A8 — keep history in time order.** The controller stamps BOTH turns of one exchange with one `now`; `AppendTo` inserts by `Timestamp` (stable sort; pairs stay adjacent because they share the stamp). Unit test: a late-landing earlier exchange is placed before the newer one.

**A9 — reopen, never re-ask.** Web `AskHeaderButton` hands `{question, sessionId}`; `BusinessSearchPage` calls `reopen(sessionId, question)` when a session id is present and `ask` only for typed text; the `ConversationGone` notice gets its own slot above the box with no Try again (M4: "press Ask"); Try again on a server error re-submits the CURRENT question only when `asked` is the one that failed. Mobile mirrors (same session). Tests: web `AskHeaderButton.test.jsx` + page test; mobile parity test.

**A10 — carry the ETag.** `IMcpSessionService.GetMemberSessionAsync` returns the ETag alongside; `AppendTurnsAsync` takes an optional known ETag for attempt 1 and re-reads only on Conflict. Saves 1 RU per ask; unit test that attempt 1 issues no read when an ETag is supplied.

**A11 — notes, no code unless the owner asks:** stall margin (15 s ping vs 20 s stall) is adequate on a healthy link; a per-tool timeout would be a new dial and a new frame — deferred with cost stated: one slow Cosmos work query today runs to the 150 s clock and ends in "took too long".

### 2.4 What the fixing session must NOT do in this lane
- Do not add a `partial` field (or any field) to `AiConversationTurn`/`AiSession` — §0.7. The flag is in-memory; the stored marker stays text.
- Do not change the marker string: web `askRules.js:67`, mobile `askRules.ts:114` and the integration test pin it.
- Do not replay tool results or citations "to save re-searching" — T7 and the per-turn re-authorization depend on NOT doing that (a document restricted between turns must vanish).
- Do not raise `SessionTurnMaxChars` as the "fix" for A-1: it raises the replay cost on every iteration; the storage/replay split is the fix.


## 3. LANE B — tool search: how it works, what is wrong, and the fix design

### 3.1 How a question reaches the business's data today (verified)

| Stage | Behaviour | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Gate 1 — offering | the model is offered only tools whose `RequiredPermission` the member holds at the tool's `MinimumScope`; three tools also need a business feature switch (`get_insights`, `search_refund_requests`, …). A tool absent for PERMISSION goes into the ACCESS block; absent for a switch into NOT SET UP | `BusinessSearchAgent.cs:105`, `BusinessSearchToolBase.IsAvailableTo/HasPermission`, `BusinessSearchToolCatalog.WithheldTopics/NotSetUpTopics` |
| Gate 2 — in code | every tool re-checks the permission before reading anything; a failure anywhere in a tool becomes `TemporarilyUnavailable` → the turn ends with the C6 error, never "nothing found" | `BusinessSearchToolBase.ExecuteAsync:77-114` |
| search_knowledge | one query per alphabet the business writes in (`BusinessSearchScriptPlan` from the facet-cached alphabet set), each leg BM25 + vector in ONE search request, filter leads with `businessId`, the per-document audience allow-list is pushed into the filter, a foreign row in the response discards the whole set and alarms; cards are rendered by the same excerpt builder the phone uses; documents are point-read only for the docs cited; images are gated on the document's visibility and minted as 30-min read SAS | `SearchKnowledgeTool.cs`, `ProviderKnowledgeSearchService.Provider.cs`, `KnowledgeDocumentRepository.ListSearchVisibleDocIdsAsync` |
| search_services | per rendering, the approved INDEX leg and the unapproved COSMOS leg run concurrently and are merged (union by serviceId); the price string is rendered server-side in the reader's culture; a draft row is flagged `published: false` with a note; the index leg asks for `LookupTooBroadThreshold + 1` rows and the service then cuts to `LookupMaxResults` (5) — the phone's page size | `SearchServicesTool.cs`, `ProviderCatalogSearchService.cs:338-356, 508-533, 817-859` |
| Group B (work tools) | each runs the PAGE's query with the page's narrowing pushed into the WHERE and the COUNT, then a per-row evaluator that can only REMOVE; `Bound(rows, trueTotal)` writes total/shown/sample note; dates are the business's own days; statuses are localized words on the card and enum names in the payload | `BusinessSearchWorkToolBase.cs`, the 12 `Tools/*Tool.cs` |
| Citations | the server mints every number per answer; a number the model invents resolves to nothing; Group A tools emit citations only for rows that survived the payload cap | `BusinessSearchCitationRegistry.cs`, `SearchKnowledgeTool.cs:191`, `SearchServicesTool.cs:278` |

Live (2026-09-06): `/approvalStatus/?` IS applied on both stamps' ProviderData containers (32 included paths; probe read the container policy) — the SKILL §19.6 / AUDIT-P4.7 §9 / AG4 / AG7 / AG11 "not applied to any stamp" statements are STALE. Measured partition-scoped: the unapproved leg costs **3.6–3.9 RU** (MEE3IC 39 services, SX3SG2 708, EFS0UC 41); the confirm-empty full scan costs **30 RU** (MEE3IC/EFS0UC) or 9 RU (SX3SG2, LIMIT hit early); the "nearest groups" GROUP BY costs **30–41 RU**. Live catalogues: CA 832 services (821 approved, 11 pending), IN 154 (136/18); **zero hourly services in either region** (so AG9-F1 `MinimumHours` is unreachable today).

|---|---|---|---|---|

| B-3 | Medium | A one-person business is still offered `list_team_members` (AUTHORIZATION-DESIGN §5 / §7 #4 "no team tools offered to solo"); the tool is gated on `team.read` only; `IsSolo` is resolved AFTER the offered list is built | `ListTeamMembersTool.cs:40` `RequiredPermission => "team.read"`; `BusinessSearchAgent.cs:105` then `:121-122`; AG5-D10#4 | live: SX3SG2 is a solo business (counter member = business) |

### 3.3 Fix design (root, class-wide; ⚑ = owner decision in §15)

**B1 — a screen-sized page and an honest cut.**
- `clinqetshared/Models/Voice/VoiceCatalogModels.cs` `CatalogLookupQuery`: add `int? MaxResults` (null ⇒ `LookupMaxResults`, the phone's 5). In-memory model, not schema.
- `ProviderCatalogSearchService.Shape(...)` takes the query and uses `query.MaxResults ?? _settings.LookupMaxResults` (both callers at :353 and :532 pass the normalized query).
- `SearchServicesTool` injects `IOptions<VoiceCatalogSettings>` and asks for `MaxResults = Math.Max(1, LookupTooBroadThreshold)` (25, the size the index already fetches); after the merge, when any usable leg has `TotalMatches > Offerings.Count`, or `TotalIsExact == false` with a filled page, the payload carries `totalMatches` (only when `legs.Length == 1 && TotalIsExact`), `showing`, and `BusinessSearchToolNotes.TooManyMatches/TooManyMatchesUnknownTotal` — the SAME shape Group B's `Bound` uses, so one truthfulness rule serves every list tool.
- Tests: `BusinessSearchCatalogRetrievalTests` — 12 matching rows → the page carries 12 rows and no sample note; 30 rows → TooBroad; a Cosmos-only stamp with `TotalIsExact` and 12 > page → `totalMatches` present; sabotage = remove `MaxResults` from the query → the 12-row test sees 5 and goes red. The voice path keeps 5 (no `MaxResults` supplied) — pin it in `Clinqet.Mcp.UnitTests` where the voice tool lives (§0.18).

**B2 — a lost leg is reported, never hidden.** `MergeCatalogLegs` returns the merged result PLUS `(int Failed, int Total)`; the tool adds `"{failed} of {total} catalogue searches did not complete"` to `notSearchedIn` (the knowledge tool's `UnnamedFailedLegs` shape), routes an empty result with failures through `PartialSearchFoundNothing`, a non-empty one through `PartialSearch`; when EVERY leg of the asked rendering failed the tool returns `TemporarilyUnavailable` (C6). Tests: index leg Unavailable + Cosmos leg 0 rows → note is `PartialSearchFoundNothing` and NOT `NothingFound` (this is the exact reproduction B); sabotage = restore the `Where(l => l.Outcome != Unavailable)` drop without the count → red.

**B3 — drop the team tool on a solo business, and keep it out of NOT SET UP.** `IBusinessSearchTool` gains a default interface member `bool HiddenOnSoloBusiness => false` (the test `StubTool` in `BusinessSearchAgentTests` needs no change); `ListTeamMembersTool` overrides it to `true` and `IsAvailableTo` returns false when `authorization.IsSolo == true`; the agent resolves `IsSolo` BEFORE the first filter whenever any tool is `HiddenOnSoloBusiness` or `CanNarrowByMember`, then filters `offered`; `BusinessSearchToolCatalog.Absent` skips a solo-hidden tool so it never lands in the NOT SET UP sentence. Tests: `BusinessSearchToolMatrixTests` — a solo owner is NOT offered `list_team_members` and the NOT SET UP block does not name "who is on the team"; a two-person business still is; sabotage = drop the `IsSolo` clause → red.

**B4 — citations follow the cap, once, in the base.** `CapPayload` returns `(string Json, int Kept)` and corrects `meta["shown"]` + adds the sample note when it cut; a new base helper `Finish(payload, rowsKey, meta, citations)` returns the `BusinessSearchToolResult` with `Citations = citations.Take(kept)`; the ten tools call `Finish` (one call site each). The convention test `BusinessSearchToolPayloadCapConventionTests` adds `Finish(` to `BoundedWriters` and a stale-row check on `NoListToCap` (AG5-D11). Test: ten 850-char review rows → `kept` < 10, citations count == kept, `shown == kept`; sabotage = `Take(rows.Count)` → red.

**B5 — pre-rendered money in every Group B payload.** One helper on `BusinessSearchToolBase`: `protected string? Money(decimal? amount, string? currency, string language)` → `CurrencyMinorUnit.TryParse(currency, out code) ? CurrencyMinorUnit.FormatMajor(amount, code, LanguageCulture.Resolve(language)) : amount.ToString("N2", culture)` (the `BusinessSearchMoney.Amount` rule, made shared); bookings/quotes/invoices/invoice_totals/offers (Fixed offers only; a percentage offer keeps `discountValue` + `"%"`) write the STRING and keep the ISO code beside it. The `MoneyNeverFormattedFromTheReadersCultureTests` guard already scans these files — the helper must be registered there as the ONE renderer, not exempted. Test: Hindi reader, INR 531000 booking → payload `total == "₹5,31,000.00"`; CAD row under an IN business → `"C$1,000.00"`; unknown code → `"1,000.00"` and no symbol.

**B6 + B18 — one name rule, localized roles.** Move `SafeName` to `clinqetcore` (static `BusinessSearchNames.Safe(displayName, membershipId)`), use it in `ListTeamMembersTool` (title AND payload) and keep the roster/redactor on it; `roles` in the payload become the localized `BusinessRole_{key}_Name` words (the model has no key map). Test: fixture with no first/last name → title == membershipId, payload never contains `@`; sabotage = revert to `Text(member.DisplayName)` → red.

**B7 — the screen's own retrieval budget.** ⚑ Either (recommended) `SearchForProviderAsync` takes its fan-out budget from `BusinessSearch:Retrieval` (rename `PerLegTimeoutSeconds` → `FanOutTimeoutSeconds`, default 8, bounded by `Answer.TotalTimeoutSeconds`; the voice overload keeps `RetrievalTimeoutMs`), or delete the dead dial (key + property + `BusinessSearchConventionTests` row + the six integration-test call sites that pass `perLegTimeoutSeconds: 20`). Measure P50/P95 retrieval on SX3SG2 (737 cards, 3 legs) before choosing the default — the probe library in §13.1 times a search request. Test: a leg slower than the budget yields `LegsSucceeded < LegsRequested` (partial note), not `Unavailable`.

**B8 — reviews: the page's rule, in one place.** ⚑ Owner ruling (§15): (a) fix the PAGE gate to `Tenant?.Has("review.read")` (recommended — every team member with review.read sees pending/rejected on the page, as the tool's comment assumed) and the tool keeps its query; or (b) keep the page Approved-only and make the tool call `IReviewService.GetPaginatedReviewsAsync(..., canSeeUnmoderatedReviews: false)`. Never a second copy of the predicate. Same dead comparison in `BroadcastProviderService.cs:393/729/1482` (self-bid guard): thread the acting user's `UserNumber` into `PlaceBidAsync` and compare against the broadcast's customer, or delete the dead guard — ⚑ ruling. Test: seed one Pending + one Rejected + one Approved review; the tool returns exactly the page's rows (AG2-06's missing golden-rule test, seeded on the real emulator).

**B9 — one scan per miss.** (a) The confirm-empty fallback (`ProviderCatalogSearchService.cs:350-356 → LookupViaCosmosAsync`) runs approved-only (`IncludeUnapproved=false`) so the two scans partition instead of overlap (the concurrent tool leg already covers unapproved); (b) memoize `NearestGroupsAsync(businessId)` per request on the Scoped service (a `Lazy<Task<…>>` field). Cost: a miss on SX3SG2 drops from ≈340 RU to ≈ 3 × (30 + 3.7) + 41 ≈ 140 RU. Measured before/after with the probe (§13.1) — write the numbers into the AUDIT-P5B close-out.

**B10 — count + top N.** `IBusinessCustomerRepository` gains `CountAsync(businessId)` (`SELECT VALUE COUNT(1)`) and `GetRecentAsync(businessId, take)` (`SELECT TOP @take * … ORDER BY c.createdAt DESC`, binding the existing `(type, createdAt DESC)` composite — verify in `CosmosContainerPolicies.cs:97-101`, no index change); the unnamed branch uses them; the narrowed branch reads only the ≤`_maxAssignedCustomerIds` reachable ids. String-shape SQL unit test (memory `feedback_cosmos_emulator_vs_prod_matcher`) + emulator test with 120 rows.

**B11 — roster tri-state everywhere.** `IBusinessSearchRosterService.TryResolveAsync` returns `(bool Read, BusinessSearchMember?)`; `ResolveScopeAsync` throws a `BusinessSearchRosterUnavailableException` on `Read == false` (the base catch turns it into C6 — no per-tool change); the agent's PEOPLE block uses `TryListAsync` and, on `Read == false`, tells the model the team list could not be read (a NEW model-facing sentence — keep it in the same style as `MemberNotResolved`; it is not measured wording, note it as such) and withholds `assignedToMemberId` from the schemas for that turn. Test: roster throws → work tool answers Unavailable, never `MemberNotResolved`.

**B12 — `Substituted` ≠ `Rejected`.** `QueryRenderings.Parse` keeps a `Substituted` list; a substituted asked-script leg is NOT added to `rejected` (it ran). Test: Gujarati question, Latin `queryInGujarati` → `notSearchedIn` is null.

**B13 — per-answer image set.** `BusinessSearchCitationRegistry` gains `bool TryClaimImage(string key)` (`docId:imageId`); `SearchKnowledgeTool` checks it BEFORE minting a SAS; the agent's cap stays. Test: two calls citing one picture → one Image frame, one mint.

**B14 — `MatchesNothing` returns before member/mine** in `ResolveScopeAsync` (three lines); `EmptyNoteFor` maps it to the team sentence. Unit test in `BusinessSearchWorkToolTests`.

**B15 — echo the narrowing.** `CatalogLookupResult.GroupIgnored` (in-memory) set by `ResolveGroupAsync` when the name did not resolve; the tool writes `filter = { group, minPrice, maxPrice, groupIgnored }` and a note when ignored. Test: unknown group → `groupIgnored == true` in the payload.

**B16 — "no profile yet" is its own note.** `IFullProviderContextService.GetPartnerContextAsync` distinguishes "no profile row" (return a `ProviderContextLookup` with `Found=false`, or a typed exception) from a failure; the three tools answer with a FeatureOff-class note ("This business has not set up its profile yet…", new model-facing constant) instead of `Unavailable`. Test: profile repository returns null → note, not C6.

**B17 — one line.** `assumed = !string.Equals(id, profile?.TimeZoneId, StringComparison.Ordinal)` in `BusinessSearchDateRangeResolver.ResolveTimeZoneAsync`. Test: stored `"Asia/Nowhere"` → `TimeZoneAssumed == true`.

**B19 — breadcrumb best-effort.** Wrap `BuildBreadcrumbLookupAsync` in a try/catch that logs a warning and returns an empty lookup (a card without a breadcrumb beats a failed answer). Test: category repository throws → the tool still returns Matches.

**B20 — one currency table, one parse.** `CountryResolutionService` resolves through `DiscoverySettings.ResolveCurrency`; delete `CurrencyByIso2`; `FullProviderContextService` and `SmartAnalyticsAggregationService` take the business currency from `ICurrencyService.GetCurrencyCodeAsync(businessId, rowCurrency: null)`; `CurrencyTotals.Resolve`, `CurrencyService.TryParseCurrency`, `ProviderConnectedAccountService:108/153`, `InvoiceMappingExtensions:151-152` all call `CurrencyMinorUnit.TryParse` (the 7-⇒-CNY class); the `= null` defaults come off `ICurrencyService` so every caller states its intent (AG9-F5). ⚑ AE→AED: drop `AE` from the three `CountryCurrencyMap`s + a convention test "every map value parses via `CurrencyMinorUnit.TryParse`" (recommended), or add `AED` to the enum + the two client twins + 20→21 currency-name keys ×10.

**B21/B22/B23 — cost items, small, do in the same pass:** memoize `HasTextAsync` per (business, doc) on the Scoped tool; point-read only image-bearing documents (the card already carries title/name); cache the inbox count projection per business (Size = 1, 30 s; the evaluator still runs per member) — or ⚑ leave B22/B23 as accepted residuals with their cost stated (per call: one projection over open conversations; per 5 min: one context build per business).

**B24/B25 — ⚑ decisions in §15:** add `minimumHours` to the services index (§0.7 table there) or point-read the ≤25 matched rows' `Pricing` from ProviderData for the index leg; add `Published` to the citation DTO (wire-only) + a draft mark on both cards + keys ×10 (needs a sheet addition — MOCKUP GATE).

### 3.4 What must stay exactly as it is in this lane (measured or owner-locked)
- The `search_services` tool description and `CatalogQueryRule` wording (measured 2026-09-05: 0/25 → 25/25) — `BusinessSearchToolCatalog.cs:22-26`, `SearchServicesTool.cs:44-51`.
- The `search_knowledge` field description (P1.5, 25/25) and the `Parse` question fallback default — `BusinessSearchQueryRenderings.cs:53-58, 86-142`.
- Both legs, unioned, with `NeedsExactTotal` waived when more than one leg runs (P4.7, −63% RU).
- The refund tool's money notes (measured 2026-09-03: 42→50/50, 48→50/50) — `BusinessSearchToolNotes.cs:139-155`.
- Gate 1 + gate 2, the registry-minted numbers, the per-turn re-authorization, the C6 rule (Unavailable is never "nothing found").


## 4. LANE C — the AI prompt: what the model is told, where it is wrong, what must not change

### 4.1 Anatomy of one model call (verified, `BusinessSearchAgent.BuildMessagesAsync` :470-560 and `ScriptRules` :559-566)

| Block | Source | Size (approx.) | Re-sent per iteration? |
|---|---|---|---|
| System prompt rules 1–9 | `BusinessSearch:Answer:SystemPrompt` (appsettings, 1,331 chars; class default byte-identical, pinned by `BusinessSearchConventionTests.TheAppsettingsBlock_MirrorsTheClassDefaults`) | 1.3 k chars | yes |
| "Answer in {language}." | `BusinessSearchLanguages.NameOf(authorization.Language)` — **the UI locale sent by the client**, not the language of the question | 20 chars | yes |
| WORK RULES (A–F) | `AppendWorkRules` — only when a Group B tool is offered; names the period vocabulary in snake_case so it matches the schema `enum` | ~1.2 k chars | yes |
| PEOPLE | roster names + ids (≤ `Members:MaxNamesInPrompt` = 60), only when a tool can narrow by member; failure → block absent (B-11) | ≤ ~3 k chars | yes |
| ACCESS | topics the member's ROLE does not reach + the exact fixed sentence `BusinessSearch_NoAccessToTopic` to say word for word | ~300 chars | yes |
| NOT SET UP | topics the BUSINESS has not switched on | ~200 chars | yes |
| History | last `FollowUpTurns×2` = 12 stored turns, verbatim (Lane A) | ≤ 48 k chars | yes |
| Alphabet block | on the USER turn (P4 C1: on the system message it appeared on one turn and vanished on the next): "TRANSLATE the question into each language … never leave a field out" | ~400 chars | yes |
| Tool list | 17 schemas, built per request (renderings per alphabet, period enum, `mineOnly`, `assignedToMemberId` only when legal) | ~6–9 k chars | yes (except the last iteration and after the tool-result budget: no tools) |
| Tool results | ≤ `ToolResultMaxChars` 6,000 each, ≤ `MaxToolResultCharsPerAnswer` 18,000 accumulated | ≤ 18 k chars | yes (accumulate across iterations) |

Model: `gpt-5.6-luna` at `reasoning_effort: none`, `MaxCompletionTokens` 1,500, `stream_options.include_usage`, no temperature; `AiModelPinConventionTests` pins the deployment name.

| `search_services` description + `CatalogQuerySubject`/`CatalogQueryRule` | `BusinessSearchToolCatalog.cs:22-26`, `SearchServicesTool.cs:44-51` | 2026-09-05: 0/25 → 25/25 (`findings/CATALOGUE-RETRIEVAL-BRAINSTORM-2026-09-05.md`) |

### 4.4 Fix design (⚑ = decision in §15)

**C1 — answer in the question's language.** The server, not the client, decides: `TextScriptDetector` already labels the question's script for the alphabet plan (`BusinessSearchScriptPlan.Build`); map the ASKED script to the answer language when it is one of the five (Gujr → gu, Deva → hi, Latn → the UI language unless the question is recognisably es/fr — Latin alone cannot separate en/es/fr, so for Latin the UI language stays the rule); `BusinessSearchLanguages.NameOf(answerLanguage)` goes into the system message; `authorization.Language` keeps driving the CARD words (citations, statuses, money grouping) — ⚑ the owner must rule whether the cards follow the QUESTION's language or the UI's when they differ (recommended: the answer follows the question, the cards follow the UI, because the cards are the screen's own controls). Tests: a Gujarati question under an English UI produces "Answer in Gujarati." in the system message (unit, `BusinessSearchAgentTests`); a live 10-question gu/hi probe records the answer language (measurement, not a wording change).

**C2 — the refusal sentence, per the sheet.** ⚑ Re-word `BusinessSearch_NoAccessToTopic` ×5 without the "Ask the business owner" clause, and format the withheld TOPIC into it (`{0}` = the topic phrase, which is model-facing English today — so either localize the 15 topic phrases ×5 or keep the sentence topic-less). Recommended: topic-less ×5 ("You don't have access to that.") — zero new keys, matches the sheet's ban, and the sentence stays "word for word" safe. This is USER-facing copy the model repeats; the measured part is the instruction AROUND it, which does not change.

**C3 — see A6** (new key ×5 + Retry-After on the Error frame).

**C4 — measure before touching.** ⚑ Re-run the P4.7 composed-schema probe (24 questions, multi-alphabet business) WITH the alphabet block present; if compliance drops, re-word `ScriptRules` to speak of "what each field asks for" instead of "the question" and re-measure 650/650 as P4 did. Until measured: no change.

**C5 — two people, one first name.** Add to rule F (the PEOPLE block) the sentence: "If two people on the list share the name the person used, do NOT pick one — ask which of them they mean, naming both." Model-facing; measure with 10 probes on a fixture roster with two "Gaurav"s (unit test pins the sentence's presence; the measurement pins the behaviour).

**C6 — with B3**, `Absent` skips `HiddenOnSoloBusiness` tools when `IsSolo == true`.

**C7 — gu/hi live probe** (AG8a-D20): 10 dispatcher questions about money in gu and hi on a fixture; count verbatim reproductions of the fixed sentence. Record in AUDIT-P5B; if the model paraphrases, the fix is to render the refusal on the CLIENT from an Error/Status frame (a code path, not an instruction) — a design change the owner rules on.

### 4.5 Prompt cost — the only lever the owner may want to pull now
Every iteration re-sends: system (1.3 k) + blocks (≤ 5 k) + history (≤ 48 k today, ≤ 16 k after A7) + schemas (6–9 k) + tool results (≤ 18 k). At 4 iterations a deep follow-up is ≈ 40–80 k tokens today and ≈ 25–45 k after A7. The 100 k/min BUSINESS window (`AIAssistant:RateLimiting:BusinessSearch:TokensPerWindow`; class default is 50 000 — AG6-F6, JSON≠class, untested) is therefore 1–2 deep follow-ups per minute per business. ⚑ The owner sets the window and the replay budget together (§15).

### 5.1 Verified CLEAN in this area (kept as evidence, not to be re-audited)
§0.6: every Cosmos operation on the Business Search path passes a partition key (AG2 §4 lists 23 sites; AG4 §A lists 33 with WHERE/ORDER BY/index binding); the base repository throws without one. Every `IMemoryCache` write on the path sets `Size` (AG4 §B, 11 sites). No `new HttpClient`, no `Task.Run` on the path, no lock over await; SSE enumerator disposal ordering intact; fire-and-forget sites all observed; the alphabet lookup is single-flighted and bounded. Session CAS, TTL, member stamp, trim — pinned (AG7 §3 table). Audience write path — catalogue-validated keys, `Deleting` refused, `Processing` allowed, CAS → 409. Duplicate-upload merge never widens, re-reads before choices, throws before teardown. Every `KnowledgeDocument` write on the feature carries `IfMatch`. C1 on all six controller routes and `voice.settings.manage` on the audience write; ten system roles hold `business.profile.read`.

## 8. The cross-currency inbox, item by item (AG12; AE→AED verified by me — B-20; live reachability from §13)

Live 2026-09-06: C1-1 REACHABLE (LXDP8G: 24 INR + 1 CAD bookings, 14 INR + 1 CAD invoices); C5-1 REACHABLE (MEE3IC provider card "from C$2" over 38 USD + 1 CAD rows; D7OZWY 3 USD + 1 INR); C5-2 LIVE (two active offers above 100 %: 20JIJQ 105 %, D7OZWY 112 %); C2 (carts) has no live rows at all; AE: 0 businesses. #### AG12 — Cross-currency inbox: verification report

Rule applied throughout (SKILL `clinqet-business-search` §17.2/§18, memory `money-one-definition-p46-2026-09-05.md`): a row's own currency is immutable and wins; the business's primary-address currency only for a NEW row / blank row; money in two currencies is never added, compared, sorted, min/max'd or labelled as one; `CurrencyMinorUnit` / `CurrencyMinorUnit.Parse` / `CurrencyTotals.Split` are the one formatter/parser/splitter; `clinqetshared/Enums/CurrencyCode.cs:11-30` is the 20-member set.

---

##### Details

**C1-1 — Bookings "Amount highest/lowest".** `BookingRepository.cs:337` `"amount" => $"c.price.totalAmount {direction}"`; web sends it (`Booking.jsx:81-82`, proven by `bookingListQuery.test.js:33-35`), mobile too (`BookingFilterSheet.tsx:44-45`). Reproduction: the owner's own contract test (`UpdateBusinessAddresses_PrimaryCountryCurrencyChanges_ReStampsServicesButNotBookings`, cited in the P4.6 memory) moves a business US→CA and keeps old bookings USD; the next booking is CAD (`BookingController.cs:1090` stamps primary). "Amount highest" then lists US$1,000 above C$900 above US$800 — one column, two moneys. Class: every `ORDER BY`/range on a money field without a currency predicate — C1-2 (invoices `c.totalAmount`, quotes `c.price.totalAmount` sort + `MinAmount/MaxAmount` filter; web `quoteServices.js:214-220` forwards `Filter.MinAmount/MaxAmount` but no page sets them — latent) and C1-3 (customer web sorts cards from many providers by `__price`, `service/[id]/page.js:59,172-173`). SoT: the row's `price.currency`. Root: money is ordered *within* its currency — `ORDER BY c.price.currency {dir}, c.price.totalAmount {dir}` (never interleaves, hides nothing), and a min/max filter requires the currency it is expressed in. §0.7 YES: composite index `(price.currency, price.totalAmount)` on bookings/quotes and `(currency, totalAmount)` on invoices in `cosmosindexsetup` (re-read `Program.cs` first per §0.6 — not opened here). Tests: string-shape SQL unit tests (memory `feedback_cosmos_emulator_vs_prod_matcher`) + Cosmos-emulator integration with two rows in two currencies asserting `[CAD 900, USD 1000]` for desc; web/mobile: none beyond the query.

**C2-1 — Cart grand total across provider groups.** `CartService.cs:420-444` sums `servicesSubTotal` over *all* groups; `:451-454` `Currency = cart.Providers.FirstOrDefault(p => !IsNullOrWhiteSpace(p.Currency))?.Currency ?? defaultCurrency`, its own comment: "A cart spanning providers in different currencies has no meaningful single total". Nothing stops two currencies in one cart (`:83-99` accepts any groups). Consumers print the number: reminder e-mail `CartReminderProcessorFunction.cs:301-304` (`FormatMajor(cart.Price.TotalAmount, Parse(cart.Price.Currency))` — "Total: C$X" where X = CAD+USD; the per-line loop at `:278-296` is per-provider and correct), web drawer `header.jsx:123` (`cartItems.reduce(... item.price * quantity)`) rendered at `:539` with `cartItems[0]?.currency`, `cartSlice.js:68-84` (same, first-found currency), mobile multi-provider checkout `CheckoutScreen.tsx:68` `allItems = providers.flatMap(g => g.items)`, `:74-78` currency from the first item, `:81-99` totals. Reachable: a CA-region cart with a CAD-primary and a USD-primary business (the country picker allows any country). Root: the cart has no single total — `CartPrice` becomes the `CurrencyTotals.Split` shape (headline currency + `Others`), e-mail/drawer/checkout render one line per currency. §0.7 YES (Cart entity/DTO shape). Tests: `CartService` unit (CAD+USD groups → no cross sum), function unit on the HTML, integration (cart save → reminder message), web/mobile jest for drawer/checkout with two currencies. Decision D3.

**C2-2 — Group currency from the profile, price from the service row.** `CartService.cs:408-411` stamps `group.Currency = profile.ResolvePrimaryCurrency(...)`; items are client-supplied (`MapToEntityItem :649-651`, `SaveCartItemDto.BasePrice`), `CartItem` carries no currency (`Cart.cs:107-177`). Live: MEE3IC (CA, 35 USD + 1 CAD services) — a US$100 service is carted, shown and booked as C$100. Root: D1 (below). Also note the server never re-reads the service to verify `basePrice` (integrity, outside this inbox).

**C2-3 — Offers region from a client currency.** `CartController.cs:282-286` parses a query-string `currency` and `:311-316` maps it to a region with `_ => "us"` — a second currency→region map beside `Discovery:CountryCurrencyMap`; any AUD/GBP business asks for US promos. Root: one resolver (region from the business's country), never from a client value. Low.

**C3-1 — Promo money caps without a unit.** `PromoCode.cs:36-41` `BudgetMinor`, `DiscountSpentMinor`, `MaxDiscountMinor`, `MinAmountMinor :86` are bare `long`; `Currency` nullable ("Required for FixedAmount" is a comment, not a rule), `Region :77-79` "null = all regions". Redemption adds the *charge's* minor units: `PromoCodeService.cs:100,103,123` `DiscountSpentMinor + discountAmount` where `discountAmount` comes from `ComputeDiscount :411-413` in `context.Currency`; `PromoDiscountMath.cs:28-30` Percentage ignores currency; `:31-33` FixedAmount returns null only `if (snapshotCurrency.HasValue && ...)` — a FixedAmount code with `Currency == null` takes `AmountMinorOff` off ₹, $ and C$ alike. Budget/min gates compare raw: `PromoCodeService.cs:309-311, 371`, `CustomerPromoService.cs:117-131`, `BookingPaymentService.cs:1879-1889`. Admin save (`ApplyPromo :1221-1249`, `InvalidPromoDiscount :1369-1397`) requires neither `Currency` nor `Region` for a money cap (compare `ValidateFeeScope :1200-1201`, which does require it for flat fees). Reproduction: Customer code, Region null, 10%, `BudgetMinor 100000` ("$1,000" — a customer promo *must* have a budget, `:1378-1379`): one ₹50,000 booking (5,000,000 minor) spends 500,000 → `promo_exhausted` for everyone after; `MinAmountMinor 5000` ("$50") admits a ₹60 booking (6,000 minor). SoT: `PromoCode.Currency` (+ `PromoCodeRedemption.Currency` already stored, `PromoCodeRedemption.cs:45`). Root: a code that names money names one currency — validation requires `Currency` when `FixedAmount || BudgetMinor/MinAmountMinor/MaxDiscountMinor != null`; eligibility returns `promo_currency_mismatch` (key exists, `:308`) whenever `context.Currency != promo.Currency` before any cap arithmetic. No new column ⇒ §0.7 NO (a SQL check constraint would be; code validation suffices). Tests: unit (mismatch rejected before budget; FixedAmount without currency rejected at upsert); integration on SQL Testcontainers (§0.8 money): two redemptions in two currencies never share `DiscountSpentMinor`. Decision D4.

**C4-1 — Location country outranks the stored currency.** `BroadcastService.cs:1893-1904` returns `ResolveCurrencyForCountry(broadcast.Location.Country, null)` *before* looking at `broadcast.Budget.Currency`; `:1906-1915` country beats `requestedCurrency`; `BroadcastProviderService.cs:1407-1428` identical. REFUTED as a defect today: `Location` is never patched (no `PatchOperation.Set("/location…")` in `BroadcastService.cs`), and `Budget.Currency` is derived from the same country at create (`:249` → `:311-319`, even the Flexible budget gets `Currency = currency`), so both resolvers return what the row stores. It is a second source of truth: if `CountryCurrencyMap` ever changes (e.g. the AE→AED fix), every stored row silently relabels. Root: read `Budget.Currency`/`LatestBid.Currency`; the map only at create; delete both `ResolveBroadcastCurrency` methods and the dead `request.Currency` seam (C4-2, an "optional currency" parameter — the exact P4.6 lesson).

**C4-2 — Bid currency.** Bids are stamped in the customer's location currency regardless of `request.Currency` (`:412, 744, 934`). REFUTED: by design (a customer reads bids in their own money) and both apps show and send the lead's currency (`leads/[id]/page.jsx:329-332`, `BroadcastDetails.tsx:392-395, 855`). P4.6's mobile fix is present.

**C4-3 — `AwardedBidAmount` stored without a unit** (`Cosmos.cs:2472-2473`); the award patch `:873-882` drops `bid.Currency`; conversion `:1380-1404` labels the amount with the resolver; DTO `:1956-1957` pairs it with `Budget?.Currency` (always set). Consistent today; fragility only. A `awardedBidCurrency` field would be §0.7 and buys nothing while C4-1's root fix stands.

**C4-4 — Customer quote form.** `QuoteCreateForm.jsx:308` `budgetCurrency = resolveCurrency(null, localeCountry?.currency, country)` (site country) while the server stamps from the job address (`BroadcastService.cs:249` `request.Location.Country`). A US-site customer posting a Canadian address types "500" under "$" and providers read C$500. Latent (whether the picker allows a foreign address — not verified). Root: the form derives the symbol from the job location's country through the same map (AppConfig), not the locale.

**C5-1 — Provider index price envelope and label.** `ProviderSearchIndexer.cs:426-433` `Currency = services.Select(s => s.Pricing?.Currency).FirstOrDefault(non-blank) ?? profile.ResolvePrimaryCurrency(...)` — the *first* service decides; `:351-362` min/max over every price of every service; `:413-424` `FromPrice` = min over all; written at `:282-285`. Card: `ProviderSearchResultMapper.cs:60-61` (`FromPrice`, `Currency`). Confirmed live: MEE3IC `minPrice 2, maxPrice 150, CAD` over 35 USD + 1 CAD rows — "from C$2". Class (C5-1b): `ProviderSearchService.cs:376-380` `fromPrice asc/desc` across providers; `AzureSearchQuery.cs:3230-3240` `PriceLow/PriceHigh` over service docs via `GetMinPrice/GetMaxPrice :3278-3290`; `SearchFilterExpressionBuilder.cs:77-101` price band on `fixedPrice/hourlyRate/startPrice` with no currency clause (a US$40 doc matches "under C$50"); `SeoLandingResultsService.cs:151-173` min/max across providers' `FromPrice`, `Currency` = first provider with a price; `dynamic-seo.js:131-144` emits bare numbers `"2 - 150"` as schema.org `priceRange` when currencies are mixed; mobile `CategoryLandingScreen.tsx:396-399` renders that range with one currency. SoT: per-row `Pricing.Currency` (each service doc already carries it — `AzureSearchIndexer.cs:1251-1253`, row ?? country, correct). Root: (a) D1 makes the provider doc's single `Currency` true by construction; until then envelope/FromPrice are computed only over rows in the doc's currency; (b) cross-business price sort/filter/range are scoped to ONE currency — the request country's via the one map — which needs `currency` filterable on both indexes ⇒ §0.7 (index attribute change). Tests: indexer unit (CAD+USD rows → envelope over the primary currency only, or fail loudly); filter-builder unit asserting the `currency eq` clause; `SearchIndexAuditFunction` guard for mixed-currency businesses.

**C5-2 — Top offer.** `ProviderSearchIndexer.cs:342-347` `OrderByDescending(o => o.DiscountValue)` across `Percentage` and `Flatrate` (`CosmosEnum.cs:22-26`; `Offer` has no currency, `Cosmos.cs:801-846`) — "₹500 off" (500) beats "40% off" (40); the card shows type+value (`mapper :63-64`) so the *choice*, not the label, is meaningless. Live `D7OZWY topOfferType Percentage / 112`: a 112% offer cannot be created now (`OfferController.cs:206-208, 311` → `DiscountValidator.ValidateOffer` → `DiscountRules.cs:41-45` `> MaxPercentage ⇒ Error_DiscountPercentageTooHigh`); `OfferValidationService.cs:324-329` documents pre-cap rows above 100 and clamps at charge time. So a customer sees "112% OFF" and is charged as 100%. Root: repair the legacy row (D5); rank the percentage family first, then flat — the rule `ProviderPromoService.cs:334-341` already applies. §0.7 NO. Tests: indexer unit for the ranking; an audit flagging `Percentage && DiscountValue > 100`.

**C6-1 — Extracted currency outranks the business AND an existing row.** `ProviderSetupServiceWriter.cs:242-245`: `if (svc.Currency.HasValue) pricing.Currency = svc.Currency.Value.ToString(); else if (existingPricing == null && ...) pricing.Currency = defaultCurrency;` — the AI path never sets `ExtractedServiceDto.Currency` (no `"currency"` in any `Services/AI` prompt/JSON; the only assignment is `CatalogManifestService.cs:212 Currency = record.Currency`), but `CatalogManifestDtos.cs:96` is `public CurrencyCode Currency { get; set; } = CurrencyCode.USD;` — non-nullable, defaulted. A dealer manifest without the field imports every record as USD under an IN/CA business, and a re-import relabels existing rows to USD (the `existingPricing` guard is on the *else* branch). This is the most plausible origin of the live USD rows (unverified). Root: nullable, no default; a record with no currency takes the business's; a record stating another currency is a mismatch to confirm — the rule the knowledge path already enforces (C6-2: `KnowledgeServiceDraftBuilder.cs:221-234` + `KnowledgeDraftApprovalService.cs:717-718` block `Error_KnowledgeDraftCurrencyMismatch`). §0.7 NO. Tests: manifest unit (missing currency → business; foreign → flagged), writer unit (existing row keeps its currency).

**C6-2 — Knowledge drafts.** `:355` new service = business currency; `:458` price change passes `service.Pricing` so the writer keeps the row's currency; foreign symbol blocks approval. REFUTED (correct). `ToExtracted(draft)` setting `Currency` not verified.

**C6-3 — A document relabels the whole catalogue.** `ProviderSetupProfileService.cs:139-150`: `if (extractedAddress != null && !IsNullOrWhiteSpace(extraction.BusinessAddress?.Country)) foreach service: if (Currency != currency) { service.Pricing.Currency = currency; Update }` — numbers untouched; the extracted address only becomes primary when none exists (`:274`), so on MEE3IC (CA primary, 35 USD rows) *any* brochure carrying an address relabels 35 USD prices as CAD silently. Siblings: `BusinessProfileController.cs:618-622` → `RestampServicesCurrencyAsync :861-882` on a primary-country change (asserted by the test named in the P4.6 memory), and — worse — `PricingMappingExtensions.cs:79` `Currency = stampCurrency` on EVERY service edit (`ServiceController.cs:474` passes `ResolvePrimaryCurrency`): fixing a typo in a USD service under a CA business relabels it CAD. This is the booking/quote re-stamp P4.6 removed ("If you cannot tell, never relabel"), still live for services, and it contradicts "row wins" while the *keep* behaviour in the AI writer (`:244`) obeys it — two rules in one codebase, and live data shows both. Root: D1.

**C6-4 — REFUTED.** `CatalogManifestService.cs:285-294` formats the deposit in the record's typed currency; the manifest country feeds service *areas* only (`:174-185`); no "country read off a PDF" path exists — the whole-catalogue relabel is C6-3. `McpService.cs:480/495/873` passes the primary currency as the writer *default* (new rows only).

**S1 — Money documents built from a service row ignore the row's currency.** New bookings/quotes are stamped `ResolvePrimaryCurrency` (`BookingController.cs:613,624,1073,1090`; `QuoteController.cs:413,424`) while `Services[].Pricing` is copied from rows; voice: `BookingTools.cs:1587-1597` `Sum(s => s.Pricing.SubTotal)` + primary, `:234,366,891,966` + `QuotePriceCalculator.cs:119` estimates in primary; `CallFollowUpApplyService.cs:438-444`. Live: a US$100 MEE3IC service books as C$100. The correct pattern already exists: `PartnerTransactionTools.cs:309,466` `Currency = service?.Pricing?.Currency ?? profile.ResolvePrimaryCurrency(...)`. Root: D1 (under one-currency-per-business these become correct by construction; under row-immutable, a booking must take — and refuse to mix — its rows' currency).

**S2** — `SmartAnalyticsAggregationService.cs:430` rollup `Currency = first service currency ?? "USD"`, `:952` blank→"USD"; `GetInsightsTool.cs:106` labels Ask Clinket answers with it. Web `InsightsView.jsx:183,192` uses per-service currency (fine); benchmarks are grouped by `(priceType, currency)` `:334-357` (correct). Latent under D1.

**S3** — `InvoiceMappingExtensions.cs:311-312` `if (dto.Currency != null) invoice.Currency = dto.Currency;` lets a client relabel an existing invoice (`UpdateInvoiceRequestDto`, `Cosmos.cs:2817-2819`); no app sends it (web `InvoiceForm.jsx:114-118` "on EDIT the invoice already states its currency and that WINS"; `app/dashboard/invoices` no `currency`; mobile none). Root: delete the DTO field (P4.6's own rule). **S4** — `:151-152` `Enum.TryParse<CurrencyCode>(...) ? pc : USD` without `IsDefined` — AG9 class, replace with `CurrencyMinorUnit.Parse`.

##### Owner decisions (with recommendation)

- **D1 — What is a service's currency?** The code holds both rules (re-stamp on edit/address/upload; keep on AI update; manifest defaults USD) and live data holds both states. **Recommend: one currency per business, achieved without silent relabel** — creation/edit stamp the business currency (already), the address-change and document-upload paths STOP relabelling and instead present "N services are priced in USD — confirm each price in CAD" (provider-confirmed conversion; mockup gate §0.7.1), manifest default removed, one-time provider-confirmed repair for MEE3IC/D7OZWY. Why: every consumer (index doc, cart group, booking, voice, insights) already assumes it; nobody sells one catalogue in two currencies; the alternative (row-immutable services) forces multi-currency into every consumer plus mixed-booking rejection. Money DOCUMENTS stay row-immutable as P4.6 ruled.
- **D2 — Cross-business/cross-row money ordering.** Composite `ORDER BY (currency, amount)` for the three work lists (§0.7 indexes) and request-country currency scope for search sort/filter/landing ranges (§0.7 index attribute). **Recommend both**; a customer filtering "under C$50" must never match US$40.
- **D3 — Cart total:** per-currency `CurrencyTotals.Split` shape vs no cart-level total. **Recommend per-currency** (§0.7 Cart shape).
- **D4 — Promo:** money-capped/fixed codes require `Currency`, mismatched charges rejected. **Recommend yes.**
- **D5 — Repair D7OZWY's 112% legacy offer; percentage-first ranking.** **Recommend yes.**

##### Siblings found beyond the inbox
C1-3, C2-3, C4-4, C5-1b (six sites), C6-1 (manifest default), the edit-path re-stamp `PricingMappingExtensions.cs:79`, S1 (nine sites), S2, S3, S4. Verified CORRECT and worth keeping as the pattern: `InvoiceRepository.cs:222,251,318` GROUP BY `c.currency`; `InvoiceService.cs:365-407, 609-646` (`CurrencyTotals`); `ProviderLedgerService.cs:313-323`, `ProviderPayoutService.cs:298-305` (`e.Currency == currency`); `AdminBillingConfigController.cs:743-759`; `BookingTaxResolver.cs:98-109`; mobile `InvoiceDueSectionHeader.tsx:29-38`; `AzureSearchIndexer.cs:1251-1253`; `PartnerTransactionTools.cs:309,466`.

##### Not verified
(1) Live reachability of a two-currency cart (no code guard; data not checked). (2) Which path wrote MEE3IC's 35 USD rows (manifest default vs pre-address creation) — needs the audit log. (3) `KnowledgeDraftApprovalService.ToExtracted` and `Currency`. (4) Whether the customer quote location picker permits a foreign-country address (C4-4). (5) Existing composite indexes for the amount sorts in `cosmosindexsetup/Program.cs` (not opened; mandatory before any ORDER BY change). (6) `SeoLandingResultsService` provider `Currency` source (assumed the provider doc). (7) Mobile partner invoice edit payload (no `currency` found; assumed none). (8) `HomepageBannerService` promo label currency. (9) No dotnet/npm/jest run (read-only mandate); scratch notes only under `…/scratchpad/AG12/notes.md`, nothing written inside any repo.

Contradicts P4.7 §1 / SKILL §19.4 "approval is the ONLY thing they differ on" | The Service row's `Pricing` (Cosmos). Either add `minimumHours` to the service index document (a search-index field ⇒ §0.7) **or** stop rendering money from the index leg: use the index for retrieval, point-read the N≤25 matched rows' `Pricing` from ProviderData, build every `CatalogPrice` from one store |

#### 2. Detail on the two HIGH findings

**F1 — evidence chain.** `SelectFields` (`ProviderCatalogSearchService.cs:48-58`) now requests `currency` (`d13222f`) and both `ToItem` overloads build `CatalogPrice` with `PriceType/FixedPrice/StartPrice/MaxPrice/HourlyRate/Currency` — that half of the P4.7 claim holds. `MinimumHours` is set only at `:555` (Cosmos). `BusinessSearchMoney.Hourly` (`:57-66`) appends `BusinessSearch_Price_MinimumHour(s)` only when `MinimumHours > 0`, so the index-served string is the shorter one. `SearchServicesTool.RunAsync` (`:127-142`) adds the index lookup first and `MergeCatalogLegs` (`:296-300`) keeps the first `ServiceId` seen; with `indexed == true` the Cosmos leg is `unapprovedOnly: true`, so an approved row has exactly one source: the index. `NormalizePriceType` maps every unknown type to `"fixed"` in both `FormatPriceText` and `BusinessSearchMoney`, so no second price-type disagreement exists. The voice `PriceText` for index rows has passed `null` minimum hours since `935c50ee` (07-28) — same class, pre-programme, not P4.7's.

#### 4. The eleven agreements — verdicts

1. **P4.6 × P4.7 overlap:** screen price obeys the P4.6 rule ✔; legs agree on currency ✔ but **not on `MinimumHours` (F1)**; `InvoiceTotalsTool` compiles and uses only the summary's currency ✔; measured `search_knowledge` wording byte-identical ✔; `Parse` keeps verification/retry/partial ✔; the `N2` fallback in `BusinessSearchMoney.cs:73` is a money-shaped `:N2` the P4.6 guard (`MoneyNeverFormattedFromTheReadersCultureTests`) does not flag because no money noun is on the line — consistent with the rule (unknown currency has no exponent) but unregistered; P4.7's audit could not have seen P4.6's `Enum.IsDefined`/`TryParse` (added `186b702` 14:35 by P4.6 into P4.7's file) — the seam was closed by the later session, correctly.
2. **recordId:** all 17 consistent (§3); nothing after P3 changed an id field.
3. **Copy keys:** clean on both apps; one web orphan (F9); no raw id can render (every rule key is mapped or screen-owned).
4. **Money:** two conventions (F2); three "business currency" resolvers (F3).
5. **Dates:** consistent; two culture-argument inconsistencies with no visible effect for the five product cultures — `PeriodOf` (`WorkToolBase.cs:323-324`) and `InvoiceTotalsTool.cs:100` format `yyyy-MM-dd` under the current culture while `Local/Day/Iso` pass `InvariantCulture`; `ListOffersTool.Iso(offer.StartDate)` and `GetInsightsTool` `DataAsOf` print a stored `DateTime` with no zone (semantics not verified, see §7).
6. **Status words:** consistent; guard gap (F11).
7. **Audience:** one decider ✔; one extra parse (F6).
8. **Currency:** stored-vs-primary precedence ✔; the map/resolver split (F3); parse copies (F4); optional parameter (F5).
9. **SSE/wire:** consistent ✔.
10. **SKILL:** eight stale claims (F8, §6).
11. **Residuals:** §5.

### 12.1 M1 · M2 · M4 · M7 · M9 (AG8a)

### AG8a — Sheet walk-through: M1 · M2 · M4 · M7 · M9, web and phone

**Method.** Every state enumerated from the five sheets (stripped to text with a node script in the scratchpad, raw markup re-read where colour/structure mattered), then located in code. Every key referenced was checked in all 5 catalogues of its platform (web `public/lang/{en-US,es-US,fr-CA,gu-IN,hi-IN}.json`, mobile `src/Locales/{en,es,fr,gu,hi}.json`, server `Resources/Localization/{en,es,fr,gu,hi}.json`) — **0 missing keys** across the 290 strings. Banned-word scan (English list + es/fr equivalents + gu/hi transliterations) over all of them: **one hit, server `Error_BusinessSearchFeedbackAnswerTooLong` "too long to record"** — a verb, and never surfaced (client fires-and-forgets, `businessSearchService.js:212-213`). "UTC" in the daily-limit sentence is drawn verbatim by M7 §04; hi server `TextUnavailable` uses "टेक्स्ट" (text), which M9 permits.

**Abbreviations.** Web: `BSP` = `clinqetwebpartnerapp/src/components/businessSearch/BusinessSearchPage.jsx`, `SC` = `SourceCard.jsx`, `SP` = `SourcePanel.jsx`, `AB` = `AskBox.jsx`, `AHB` = `AskHeaderButton.jsx`, `SAM` = `SearchAnswerMarkdown.jsx`, `hook` = `src/hooks/useBusinessSearch.js`, `rules` = `src/lib/businessSearch/askRules.js`, `svc` = `src/services/businessSearchService.js`. Phone: `BSS` = `clinqetmobilepartnerapp/src/Screen/ProfileFlow/BusinessSearch/index.tsx`, `SCm` = `components/businessSearch/SourceCard.tsx`, `SS` = `SourceSheet.tsx`, `AAM` = `AskAnswerMarkdown.tsx`, `hookm` = `src/hooks/useBusinessSearch.ts`, `rulesm` = `src/lib/businessSearch/askRules.ts`. Server: `Agent` = `clinqetinfrastructure/Services/BusinessSearch/BusinessSearchAgent.cs`, `Ctrl` = `clinqetapi/Clinqet.API/Controllers/BusinessSearch/BusinessSearchController.cs`, `SKT` = `clinqetinfrastructure/Services/BusinessSearch/Tools/SearchKnowledgeTool.cs`, `DTO` = `clinqetshared/DTOs/BusinessSearch/BusinessSearchDtos.cs`. Web keys are `BusinessSearch.*`; phone keys are the `BUSINESS_SEARCH.*` twins (mapped at `BSS:747-782`, `SCm:43-55`); "×5 ✓" = present in all five files of that platform.
